Questions 151-152 refer to the following information. #### Holiday Bus Timetable Buses on Route 181 will operate on an amended schedule during the holiday season to accommodate public and private events. The route begins at Bobcat Stadium, running through the downtown area and terminating at Grenada Plaza. The last bus usually departs the stadium at 10:00 P.M. on weekdays and 10:30 P.M. on weekends. From December 29 to January 3, it will run later than usual, with the last bus departing at 11:00 P.M. on December 29–31 and at 11:45 P.M. on January 1–3. 151. Why was the information written? From: Tonya Keiper To: Sales representatives Date: July 14 Subject: Trek The Lorimor R&D team has finalized the latest version of our GPS navigation system, Lorimor Trek 2.0. There are several differences between this model and the previous one. We've added a voice-activated command center, which will allow drivers to perform operations without using the touchscreen. We've also made the dashboard unit slightly larger, and there is a special coating on the screen to reduce glare. We were able to use a lot of ideas from after-purchase feedback shared by our customers who are using the original Lorimor Trek. We already have a detailed user manual ready for the system, but next week I'm going to try to make it shorter so that users can read through it quickly and start using the Lorimor Trek 2.0 right away. Please be prepared to explain the differences to customers. 153. Why did Mr. Keiper send the memo? The phrase “break down” in paragraph 1, line 3, is closest in meaning to (A) malfunction (B) divide (C) get upset (D) slow downRICHMOND (August 16)—Valley Hardware Store has announced that it will move to the Hillside neighborhood next month. The hardware store's new home will be in the former Orton Department Store building on Ramsey Avenue. The store's owner, Yolanda Joyce, hopes that the new site will better serve customers. "People are trying do-it-yourself projects at home more frequently these days instead of relying on tradespeople who do those kinds of projects for a living. — [1] —. So, they need the right materials to get the job done, and we need more space to provide what they need," Joyce said. — [2] —. The business's employees will regularly show shoppers how to carry out simple projects such as varnishing furniture, removing paint, and installing curtain rods. "We want to give our customers the support and confidence they need to get the job done," said general manager William Clifton. — [3] —. Another change on the horizon is an intensive training program for employees. “Through our most recent market research, we learned that customers buy more when they are assisted by staff members who are well-acquainted with the products and their uses,” added Clifton. — [4] —. 168. In which of the positions marked [1], [2], [3], and [4] does the following sentence best belong? “A portion of the sales floor will be left empty for demonstrations.” (A) [1] (B) [2] (C) [3] (D) [4]##### The Sweet Taste of Success STREAMWOOD (9 November)—Located on a small street in a quiet town, a little bakery has begun to get noisy. Gilmore's Cakes has become so famous in such a short time that people are requesting cakes for their events up to as much as a year in advance. One thing that sets this bakery apart from others is that every cake is different. Depending on the occasion, owner and head baker Samuel Gilmore changes something to make it fit the event. It could be the color, the decorations, or even individual taste preferences. Mr. Gilmore says that he got the idea for his business from his family. "When I was growing up, my grandma would always make a special birthday cake for family members on their birthdays," he explains. "After attending culinary school, I wanted to create a personal touch that customers would be able to enjoy." When Gilmore's Cakes opened three years ago, it did not enjoy the kind of attention that it does today. Some locals enjoyed its products, but special orders were few and far between. After unsuccessful attempts of advertising in newspapers and on local radio, Mr. Gilmore decided to try a more modern technique by offering special deals to customers who posted about his business on social media. Within weeks, images of cakes made by Mr. Gilmore were all over the Internet and the demand for his services have skyrocketed. The American Culinary Committee (ACC) has invited Mr. Gilmore to its headquarters in Bakersfield to be officially recognized as this year's National Cake Master. The award ceremony will be attended by celebrity chef Mila Baldwin and broadcasted live on Edibles Network.172. (A) Join a committee (B) Receive an award (C) Host a television show (D) Judge a competition### Community Spotlight on Ashley Gilbert Interviewed by Earl Schultz Excerpt below from May 3 interview with Ashley Gilbert, who is offering a new class in Mountainview. Earl Schultz: Cooking has always been a popular hobby. How does your class tie in with that? Ashley Gilbert: Well, in the digital age, people are looking for ways to share their creations on social media, blogs, and more. I want to help people capture their food presentation in a professional way. ES: I love cooking, and I'm interested in photography, too. How can your class help a complete beginner like me, who doesn't know anything about taking photos of food as an art form but wants to learn how to do it better? Would I have to buy a high-end camera to participate in the class? AG: Not at all. In the very early days of my photography business, I was getting by with only an inexpensive used camera I bought from a Web site. ES: That's reassuring for people who aren't sure whether they want to fully commit to this hobby. AG: Exactly. Food photography is less about the device you're using and more about how you present the subject matter in the composition and framing. Lighting also makes a big difference. In fact, that's why I'm so pleased my class will be held in Braxton Hall, which has a lot of natural light as well as a modern lighting system.
Mountainview Community Events: Classes in June
Dessert Creations\nDate & Time: June 9, 2 P.M.–4 P.M.\nLocation: Greenway Plaza\nDetails: Learn decorating techniques for beautiful cakes and other desserts that are worthy of a blog post. [Fee: $35]
Growing Herbs\nDate & Time: June 15, 10 A.M.–12 Noon\nLocation: Timberbrook Institute\nDetails: Grow your own herbs in your garden to add to your homemade recipes. Complimentary sun hat given to all participants. [Fee: $25]
Food on Film\nDate & Time: June 20, 9 A.M.–11 A.M.\nLocation: Braxton Hall\nDetails: Learn how to take professional-style photos of your food. All experience levels welcome. [Fee: $25]

Sure Storage, conveniently located on Detroit Avenue, is the solution for you! At Sure Storage, you can select a rental period as short as one month. This is the perfect solution for people who are moving to a new home or who need items temporarily out of their home during renovations. Our security team works day and night to ensure that no unauthorized parties access your unit. In addition to walk-throughs of the facility, we also have numerous monitoring screens so that we won't miss anything. Along with the size, customers can choose from a range of services with different amenities. Our standard service, the Bronze Service, is for storage only near the rear of the facility. With the Silver Service, customers can get assistance with on-site loading/unloading. Our Gold Service includes on-site loading/unloading as well as heaters and air conditioners within the unit. Our Diamond Service includes everything in the Gold Service and the storage of up to two vehicles. Prospective customers are invited to stop by the site anytime for a facility tour. Please direct any inquiries you may have to Jackie at jdavison@s-storage1.com.

http://www.alvaradoco.net/notices Home Products $ \underline{\text{Notices}} $ FAQ ##### Product Notice: Home Climate Control Unit AP70 If you own an AP70 home climate control unit, you may have had some difficulties when changing the air filter. Several members of our repair team have reported similar problems after visiting the homes of customers who have this model installed. The cover part of the unit that unlocks and locks so that the air filter can be changed is extremely fragile. We have concluded that this is a design flaw, and as such, we will not invalidate the warranty if that is the only part that has been damaged. A member of our repair team will replace the part free of charge. We understand that some of you may want a refund for your AP70. If that is the case, you will be responsible for shipping or delivering it to the nearest repair center to you. Our locations can be found on our Web site. Upon receipt of your AP70, our Customer Service Team will process a refund based on your product registration information. This notice only applies to AP70. There have been no design flaws found with either the AP80 or AP90 home climate control units.##### ☐☐☐ To: Customer Service Team From: Lisa Shepherd Date: 29 August Subject: AP70 Dear Customer Service Team, I purchased an AP70 home climate control unit last year in June, and the first time I tried to change the filter as instructed in the owner's manual, some parts of the filter cover broke off. Although they studied unrelated majors, they became friends as members of the Schenectady Cycling Student Club. They started discussing ideas to turn their shared hobby into a network, but it wasn't until several years after opening SpinShop that they were able to establish SpinServe. — [1] —. SpinServe is now a network of over 70,000 members, nearly 80% of whom are currently attending high school or college. — [2] —. It connects event planners to volunteers who can ride their bicycles to help out. The two young entrepreneurs recruited Edward Mahoney to design and monitor the mobile app. He is a current member of the club that Ms. French and Ms. Powers once belonged to. — [3] —. The acquisition is the first step in the company's expansion into North America. Swoboda has one of the country's largest networks of storage facilities and transportation systems, which will be invaluable for distributing VT's goods throughout North America. Swoboda also produces a line of its own lighting for offices, hospitals, retail enterprises, and more, nicely complementing VT's line of residential lighting. "We want to expand to other areas, but it's important for us to make sure we are on top of the logistics. That's why we're pleased to acquire Swoboda, as it is locally based and its employees know the regulations better," said Daniel Murray, a spokesperson for VT Lighting. This deal marks the first acquisition by VT Lighting, but it certainly won't be the last. The company is already looking into making a similar arrangement when it expands into South America next year. With steady growth, VT is well positioned to become a worldwide market leader. 田 165. In which of the positions marked [1], [2], [3], and [4] does the following sentence best belong? “Attendees will then have time to ask him any questions they may have.” (A) [1] (B) [2] (C) [3] (D) [4]#### Springfield Tribune SPRINGFIELD (Monday, May 14)—If you were to view the Velex Building from above, you'd see that it is bursting with vegetation, color, and energy. The building's owner, Jacob Feldman, has just completed a project to add a rooftop garden to the building, thanks to the Community Enrichment Grant, which is awarded annually by the Harper Foundation. The grant has been awarded for Springfield-based projects twice in the past, once for renovation work at St. Mary Chapel, which dates back to the 1700s, and once for repairs at the former City Hall building, which was used by city employees over 150 years ago. Mr. Feldman applied for the grant last year, but it was the changes to the design and the addition of a public access component that finally gave him a successful grant proposal this year. The rooftop features benches and a small picnic area for Springfield residents, surrounded by planting beds full of beautiful flowers and fragrant herbs. A collection of photos of the garden, and additional information, will be printed in next week's edition of Week Around Town. ##### ☐☐☐ To: Jacob Feldman From: Edith Landers Date: Tuesday, May 23 Subject: Rooftop garden Dear Mr. Feldman, I saw photos of your building's rooftop garden in the local magazine that came out today. However, as classes are not scheduled around the clock, these rooms often sit empty for a large proportion of the day. Some gym owners are looking for ways to make these spaces useable so that they can generate income even when fitness classes are not being conducted. Theo Tremblay, the owner of Iron Gym, has recently launched a new program called Assembly-360. Through the program, groups in the area—such as community clubs and small businesses—can rent the unused classrooms on an hourly basis for meetings and training events. book far in advance just to get a spot. With Assembly-360, they have more options available," said Tremblay. “There is a significant lack of public meeting spaces in Littleton, so people often have to So far, Iron Gym already has had over twenty bookings through the program. Tremblay has invested in tables and chairs that can be set up and taken down quickly, converting the room from a workout space to a meeting room. In addition, the padded walls provide excellent soundproofing so that meeting attendees are not disturbed by the other gym sounds. To further improve the experience of meeting participants, Tremblay provides hot coffee and tea along with some popular snacks as part of the service.” “I was worried about the atmosphere of this space,” said Marsh Wilson, who held a meeting at Iron Gym last week, “but I was pleasantly surprised by how the room was transformed.” 161. The historical building, dating back to the 1800s, is in need of extensive repairs. After the chapel was on the market for two years, the city of Stockton purchased the building and assumed responsibility for the restoration. Initially, locals were in favor of the purchase, but the restoration project has not moved forward as promised. "I fully support saving old buildings, but the city needs to take action. The houses in this neighborhood are well-cared for by their owners, and this is a stark contrast to the chapel," said Pablo Cavillo, who lives near the chapel. “I don't understand why construction hasn't begun yet. I'm tired of seeing this eyesore every day. Someone needs to fix this mess,” added Kelly Garner. Mayor Rhoda Moore was reluctant to purchase the building at first, but he was persuaded to go ahead with the project after a city-employed financial consultant convinced him that it would be good for tourism. Moore plans to hold a meeting next month to discuss the chapel and hear opinions from the public.172. (D) He teaches classes about films.#### Price Jump at Racine Semiconductors January 3—Racine Semiconductors has announced plans to increase the sale price of its goods. The increase will affect both its silicon-based products and those containing other materials. The change will result in price increases of 3 to 7 percent, which may, in turn, drive up the price of consumer goods that contain electronic circuits. Racine Semiconductors has three plants throughout Arizona—its main plant in Tucson, and two smaller plants in Mesa and Glendale. Though profits have been steady over the past few years, the company has been cited on several safety violations. The price changes will help to create enough of a surplus to invest in better protective gear as well as increase the amount of training in an effort to decrease workplace injuries. “We strive to create the best product while also taking care of our workers. This price change will allow us to do that,” said Debbie Jorgensen, a company spokesperson. (A) Paid vacation time (B) Health insurance (C) Unpaid sick days (D) A three percent sales bonus ### Start-Up Business Seeks to Reduce Effect of Fast Fashion By Stephen Hampton BRANCHBURG (January 8)—With the new year comes a new idea for helping the environment. The start-up company C-Loop is buying and selling used clothing in Branchburg. Janet Roscoe got the idea for C-Loop after watching a documentary on fast fashion. "These days, people can buy clothing cheaply, wear it for a short time, and then throw it away. This is very harmful to the environment," Roscoe said. C-Loop, which stands from "Clothing Loop", is a way for residents of Branchburg to get the most out of their clothing. They can download the C-Loop smartphone app to view the items of clothing that are currently available. With just a few taps, their order will be sent to their home, as long as it is within the Branchburg city limits. They can also sell items and receive credit in their account. In addition to saving clothing from heading straight to the landfill, C-Loop also reduces the amount of new clothing that needs to be sold in town. This is another way to help the environment, as there will be fewer trucks transporting goods the long travel distance from neighboring towns. You can sign up for a C-Loop account with just a five-dollar deposit by visiting www.c-loop.com. 161. Who is Janet Roscoe? (A) An environmental scientist (B) A filmmaker (C) A business owner (D) A clothing manufacturer 163. What is required to set up a C-Loop account? (A) A valid driver's license (B) A credit card (C) A landline phone number (D) A deposit payment 162. What is suggested about C-Loop? (A) Its service is based in the Branchburg area. (B) It has stores throughout the nation. (C) It delivers clothing to the neighboring towns. (D) It regularly distributes a newsletter to its users.

Mark Hamlin of the Georgia Times interviewed the owner of Ashburn Café, Donna Combs, about the café and its continuous prosperity. MH: For the past decade, Ashburn Café has been a landmark for tourists and locals alike. What is the secret to your business' success? DC: Well, we have always done our best to provide quality baked foods and beverages at reasonable prices. Of course, every café does that, though. I think that we were really able to stand out from the others by offering free items to customers who made positive posts about our café on social media. Social media marketing wasn't nearly as big as being back then, but it really took off. MH: You mentioned that you sell baked foods and beverages. As a long-time regular customer, I see that the menu changes regularly. How do you decide what to keep and what to add? DC: Our menu also gets a lot of influence from social media. The items that are most commonly posted about are kept, and we always listen to suggestions and requests from our customers. Our bakers and baristas are always coming up with new ideas based on our customers' input. That's how we come up with new additions. Some stay for years, and some get replaced before long. 164. Graduating from Australian National University (ANU), Ms. Martz was immediately accepted into an internship program at Hubbard Co. While there, she created an online course for financial advisors that is still used today. She worked her way up to a management position and was then recruited by Landry Financial to oversee their operations in Philadelphia, and she has been working there for the past seven years, most recently as the COO. Ms. Martz will replace Rivard's current CEO, Timothy York, in June when he leaves the company for retirement.

Offering cruises going between Florida and the Caribbean, it is already receiving considerable praise from its passengers. The company was formed as a subsidiary of Comfort Tours with the intention of appealing to wealthier customers. While Comfort Tours offers many affordable vacation packages, Pearl Cruises creates the ultimate luxury vacation. CEO Brian Conner pointed out that the market for vacations and tourism is becoming more and more aimed for the upper class elite. “The average person is too busy with work and covering normal expenses these days,” Mr. Conner said. “To make real profits, you have to go to where the money is.” Pearl Cruises is offering a special promotion throughout the month of April for customers who pay for a reservation with a Cruiser Credit Card by charging no interest. ## 1 ##### Pearl Cruises Reservation Confirmation Departure: 16 April Return: 21 April Port: Miami Name(s): Alison Schroeder Accommodation: Royal Room Payment Type: Cruiser Credit Card xxxx-xxxx-xxxx-8313 $ ^{*} $Your full itinerary is being mailed to you along with your boarding pass. $ ^{*} $

There are three parts, and directions are given for each part. You are encouraged to answer as many questions as possible within the time allowed. This product contains harsh chemicals, so it should be kept away from children at all times. If possible, make sure the room is well-ventilated prior to use. 1. Slowly pour 15 ounces of Casper Drain Cleaning Gel directly into the drain without diluting it. Please note that the gel will not harm the pipes, but it can cause damage to countertops and floor surfaces. — [2] —. 2. Let the gel sit in the drain for at least twenty minutes to allow it to work. If there is a major blockage, let it sit for at least forty-five minutes. — [3] —. 3. Flush the pipe with very hot, but not boiling water until the pipe is flowing well. — [4] —. Repeat Steps 1 and 2 if necessary. 4. Unused contents can be kept for at most a year, after which time, the ingredients may be less effective. Keep the bottle away from all heat sources while in storage. 158. What can affect how long the product should be left in the drain? We have adjusted the way that we deal with ticket cancellations for our bus trips. Previously, we did not charge for cancellations made before the travel day. (A) Proofread a document (B) Contact Mr. Irwin (C) Post something online (D) Visit a manager's office#### Boating in Minnesota Minnesota is the “Land of 10,000 Lakes,” so it offers many opportunities to enjoy the water. However, before you take your boat out, you must ensure that it is properly registered by the Parks and Wildlife Department. Please adhere to the following regulations: 1. Registration is required for all motorized boats and all boats that exceed 12 feet in length before they are used in public lakes. This should be done at the county office in which the titleholder resides, regardless of where the boat is being stored. 2. If you are transferring the possession of a new or used boat, either through sale or by gift, the new registration must be updated within 45 days. Failure to do so may result in a fine imposed on both parties. 3. The registration cannot be transferred to another boat. It is valid for a period of twelve months. The fee charged for registration varies from $25 to $60, depending on the length of the boat. A complete list of fees is available on the Parks and Wildlife Department's Web site. 4. Visitors from out of state do not have to register their boats in Minnesota if they are already registered in the home state. They can use public waters in Minnesota for up to 60 consecutive days without registration. After that time, a guest permit will be needed. Like the standard registration, it can be obtained from any county office.172. Who is the intended audience of this information? (D) They do not need to register for the first two months.#### Vista Heights Property to be Transformed SUMMITVILLE (January 13)—Work has begun on a city property in the southern neighborhood of Vista Heights. Following years of planning, the groundbreaking began yesterday on a 20,000-seat stadium that will host baseball games, football tournaments, concerts, and more. Because of the population growth in Summitville over the past decade, the city is now large enough to support a sports venue of this size. The project will cost an estimated $1.8 million and it is expected to yield tens of thousands of dollars in tax revenue for the city annually and give a boost to local businesses. The stadium's main entrance will be on Maxwell Street and will include bronze statues of sports legends in an outdoor sculpture garden. The west entrance, on Everette Street, will be the site of an underground parking lot. The decision to put the parking lot underground was made because the historic Knowlton Manor is opposite the stadium on that street, and planners did not want to obstruct the view. City council members have also suggested changing Woodall Street, which runs along the northern end of the stadium, to pedestrian-only access. However, this proposal has not been finalized yet. ##### Dear Summitville City Council Members: I am writing to you regarding the new project in the Vista Heights neighborhood. I operate a jewelry store that is right across the street from the stadium's site, on Everette Street. While I will appreciate the additional foot traffic that the stadium will bring, I also have some concerns about safety. Everette Street is a very busy roadway, and visitors to the stadium may be unfamiliar with the area, trying to cross the road without using the designated crosswalks. Would it be possible to change the speed limit on this street from 35 miles per hour to 20? I think this may be the easiest way to avoid potential injuries. Thank you for your consideration, Sincerely, William Escobar Owner, Sparkle Jewelry176. What is the article mainly about? (A) To sign up for a tour (B) To inquire about group rates (C) To suggest an activity (D) To become a volunteer ##### Exciting News for Outdoor Enthusiasts WHITESTONE (March 22)—A proposal to deal with the neglected pond at the northern end of Amato Park has been finalized, city officials confirmed this morning. A wooden walkway will be added around the pond, and signs to teach people about wildlife will be posted along the route. Whitestone residents were overwhelmingly in favor of the news, despite the high cost of the project. “It’s difficult for people to enjoy the pond in its current state,” said Maria Sheley, who lives near the park. “By adding a walkway, everyone will be able to get great views of the pond without getting muddy or disturbing the vegetation growing near the water. It’s worth every penny.” The work will not only help visitors to the park but animals as well. As part of the project, researchers will work toward reversing the effect that pollution has had on the pond. They will introduce plants that act as natural filters to help keep the water clear. "Before and After" pictures of the project will be posted on the city's Web site. For more information, contact the Parks and Recreation Department. 161. Why was the article written? (D) She thinks a fee is not reasonable.SOUTHDALE (November 18)—The Southdale Tigers baseball team announced at a press conference yesterday that the Southdale Stadium will be known as Meridian Stadium from January 1. The naming rights for the stadium were sold to food industry giant Meridian Foods. Meridian executives hope the move will help to not only support local sports but also promote its efforts to branch out into the energy drink market, as a new line of these goods hit supermarkets just a few weeks ago. The money raised from the naming rights will be directed to a number of areas. Some of the funds will be used to increase the salaries of the players, and the team's owners will be given a share of these funds. The team will also purchase a new bus that will be used for trips shorter than 200 miles. However, ongoing projects—such as fixing worn seats and purchasing equipment upgrades for the concession stands—will still be funded by revenue from tickets only. Elsie Blanchard, who oversaw the legal aspects of the deal on behalf of the team, said she was pleased with the outcome of the negotiations and that the contract for naming rights will be up for renewal after five years. 168. Unlike other cleanup projects undertaken by the club in recent years, this one is also aimed at raising money to support a local literature campaign. The club's members will be picking up litter at the beach all day on Saturday, May 18. — [2] —. The RCC is asking for donations from local businesses for each pound collected. “Belhaven Beach is one of the few places in the area where visitors can feel like they are a part of nature. — [3] —. We wanted to do something to change that,” said RCC President Billie Wein. “To help our cause, businesses can donate as little as five cents per pound of collected trash. Of course, we welcome much more than that. Each business can decide for themselves what amount would be suitable for their own situation.” All money raised by the project will go toward the purchase of books, with a goal of purchasing at least five hundred of them. — [4] —. These will be distributed to students at Rosedale Elementary School. The books will be theirs to keep, and RCC members hope that their efforts will encourage children to read more over the summer break. After seeing the success of selling reusable cups for coffee at his local coffee house, owner Tony Rossi got the idea to apply this practice to his restaurants. The Mega Box Program, the first of its kind among pizza restaurants, will give customers the option of purchasing a reusable aluminum pizza box for their take-out orders. Rossi hopes the program will help to drastically cut down on the number of cardboard pizza boxes that are thrown away, as these cannot be recycled due to their absorption of grease. To participate in the program, customers can purchase boxes at any Tony's Pizzeria branch. When they use the boxes to take home pizzas, they can get fifty cents off their order for each box used. "We're giving more of a discount than we save from the unused box, but the important thing is to create opportunities for sustainable practices," Rossi said. Since the boxes are made out of aluminum, they retain heat well and are easy to recycle after they can no longer be used. The company's Web site features a page explaining the benefits of the program as well as information about how to wash and store the boxes to keep them in good condition. Rossi expects business to pick up once customers hear about the change, as consumers are becoming more active in supporting businesses with environmentally friendly practices. 1 168. ##### READING TEST In the Reading test, you will read a variety of texts and answer several different types of reading comprehension questions. The entire Reading test will last 75 minutes. There are three parts, and directions are given for each part. You are encouraged to answer as many questions as possible within the time allowed. You must mark your answers on the separate answer sheet. Do not write your answers in your test book. ##### PART 5 Directions: A word or phrase is missing in each of the sentences below. If you plan on transporting more than two passengers, you will need a separate vehicle. ##### Vernon Moving Vans: Roadside Assistance Round-the-clock roadside assistance is available for all vans rented from Vernon Moving Vans. Should you experience an issue with your vehicle, call us toll-free at 1-800-555-4433. We will dispatch a mechanic to attempt to make repairs. If he or she is unsuccessful after thirty minutes, a replacement vehicle will be supplied to you, and our staff will help you to move your items to the new vehicle. For minor repairs that you are able to handle yourself, such as replacing a flat tire, please call us first to inform us of the change. Then we can have our mechanics double-check the work carefully when the vehicle is returned to one of our branches.To: Kevin Lefebre From: Vernon Moving Vans Date: June 22 Subject: Follow up I would just like to follow up on the call we had earlier today. (D) Mr. Shields learned them in the college.# NOTICE TO GREENWOOD LIBRARY PATRONS Last month, the Greenwood Library received a donation of an extensive collection of rare stamps from a local supporter's estate. After considering the library's financial needs and its space limitations, the Greenwood Library Advisory Committee (GLAC) has determined that the best option is to sell some of the stamps to raise funds for the library. An auction will be held at the library on Saturday, February 4, in Lecture Room 1. Very rare and valuable stamps will be sold individually, and those that are somewhat rare will be sold in groups of 6-10 stamps. The Greenwood Museum of History will accept the leftover stamps and make a permanent display for them to replace its existing Pioneer Life exhibit. We're seeking volunteers to assist with the auction. We'll need people to pass out flyers around town on Saturday, January 28. We'll also need people to set up tables and displays on February 3 and to keep records of financial donations on February 4. For more information, e-mail Collette Robbins at c.robbins@greenwoodlib.org. ##### ☐☐☐

##### Dear Mr. Martinez, Thank you for your interest in our upcoming stamp auction. Regarding your question, we will only be selling stamps that are in mint or near-mint condition, so you can be assured of the quality. The stamps had been mounted in special stockpages, which means that they were held in place by a clear plastic pocket rather than being stuck to the page. You can see photos of the stamps that will be up for auction on the library's Web site. I love your idea of adding these stamps to your mixed media artwork, so I hope you find some that will work well for your project. I'd also like to thank you for offering to be a volunteer. I'll see you in person when you report for volunteer duty at the library on January 28. Please arrive by 10 A.M. Sincerely, Collette Robbins
